## assistant

Saved. The turn is deeper than I proposed: the monitor leaves the frame apart from a sliver at most, and a short "don't look" cue goes on screen during the turn. I'll draft the wording for approval later.

Now the plate, because a turn that deep cannot come from the current still. The monitor sits at the left of `room-v3.png`, so turning away from it means turning right, and there is no room to the right of that image. Three ways to get one:

- **Outpaint the current still** into a wide panorama, then the Remotion camera pans across it. Same room, same light, cheapest, but a panorama pan is a flat slide, not a head rotation. It will look like a camera on rails.
- **Generate a first-person Kling clip** from the current still, "head turns right from the monitor to the bookshelves and back". Real parallax, the desk edge and monitor bezel move at different rates, which is what actually sells "through the eyes". Costs about 19 credits per attempt of your 641, and I usually need two or three attempts to get a clean one.
- **Build the room in 3D** and render the real head move. Full control, matches every cut, but it's a rebuild of the room and the "realistic, real life" bar is hardest to hit this way.

**Q3. Which one, and does the same turn get reused for every retell, or does each of the seven passages get its own?**

My suggestion: Kling clips, generated in both directions, turn-away and turn-back, and reused for all seven retells. Reuse is not a compromise here. A student turns their head the same way every time, and a repeated move reads as a habit, which is what you're teaching. Budget cap of about 100 credits for the whole plate set, and I stop and show you before spending past that.

## user

Make sure it feels natural. You should have something like "Look Away" on screen, in a 3D script or text effect, in this free space. Look to the side, but not too dramatically. Use the coolest effects possible for the most realistic motion, and then it comes back. Also make sure that it should not remake the room. It should just be a first-person view but looking to the side. It should also be very high-end design. This is going to be a sample I use forever. You don't have to show the face of the person or anything like that, so just make it clear he's looking away with whatever action he's doing. It can be blurred to the side when he looks in the corner, or it could be in the corners of his eye. The screen should be centered, so it should be straight in front of them and then look away in a way that is most effective.

## assistant

Saved. Three things locked from that: the room is not remade, "Look Away" is the cue and it lives as 3D text in the space the turn reveals, and the screen is centred straight ahead before the turn. That last one closes the Round 6 centring question. I'll extend the current still sideways so the monitor lands at centre without regenerating the room, then build the turn from that. I also noted that corner-of-eye blur is now allowed as an optical effect, which loosens the Round 6 "no blur" rule for the turn only. Masking blur on a screen you're facing stays banned.

You didn't say whether one turn gets reused across all seven retells. I'm assuming yes unless you object.

Next branch is The Recall, because it's the longest look-away and it changes what the free space has to hold.

**Q4. During The Recall, does the head stay turned for the whole 20 to 25 seconds while the argument rebuilds in that free space, or does it come back to the blank screen partway?**

In the app, the screen goes blank for the Recall, so there is nothing to look at either way. My suggestion: the head turns and stays turned for the entire recall, and the recall stack becomes the 3D text in the room, building sentence by sentence next to "Look Away". The turn-back only happens at the end, when the two Space presses fire. That gives one long, calm hold instead of a head that keeps wandering, and it keeps the stack off the monitor, which was one of your bubble complaints.

The alternative is head back to the blank screen and the stack rebuilding on it, which is closer to what the app shows but puts text on the monitor again.

/*
 * Mapping the replicated app screen onto the monitor in the generated plate.
 *
 * The quad below was DETECTED from public/plates/room-v3.png, not eyeballed:
 * the screen is the largest light, low-saturation region in the image, and its
 * four extreme corners were taken from that mask. Left edge 1936px, right edge
 * 1668px, so the monitor is turned 13.8% away from the camera. Any new plate
 * must be re-detected, never adjusted by hand.
 */

export type Pt = readonly [number, number];

/**
 * Screen corners in room-v3.png, normalised 0..1. TL, TR, BR, BL.
 *
 * Pushed out from the detected centroid by OVERSCAN. The detector finds the
 * light region, which stops a pixel or two inside the bezel, and the first
 * render showed a grey sliver of the original plate down the right edge. The
 * composite must overshoot into the bezel, never fall short of it.
 */
const DETECTED: readonly [Pt, Pt, Pt, Pt] = [
  [0.0712, 0.1198],
  [0.6156, 0.1839],
  [0.6052, 0.7266],
  [0.0752, 0.7500],
] as const;

const OVERSCAN = 1.042;

export const SCREEN_QUAD: readonly [Pt, Pt, Pt, Pt] = (() => {
  const cx = DETECTED.reduce((a, p) => a + p[0], 0) / 4;
  const cy = DETECTED.reduce((a, p) => a + p[1], 0) / 4;
  return DETECTED.map(
    ([x, y]) => [cx + (x - cx) * OVERSCAN, cy + (y - cy) * OVERSCAN] as Pt,
  ) as unknown as readonly [Pt, Pt, Pt, Pt];
})();

/** The plate is 5504x3072 (1.792:1), slightly wider than the 16:9 frame. */
export const PLATE_W = 5504;
export const PLATE_H = 3072;

/**
 * CSS matrix3d that maps an axis-aligned w x h element onto an arbitrary quad.
 *
 * Solves the projective transform taking the unit square to (p0,p1,p2,p3),
 * then post-multiplies by scale(1/w, 1/h) so the element's own pixel size is
 * absorbed. Requires transformOrigin "0 0" on the element.
 */
export function quadTransform(
  w: number,
  h: number,
  p0: Pt, p1: Pt, p2: Pt, p3: Pt,
): string {
  const [x0, y0] = p0, [x1, y1] = p1, [x2, y2] = p2, [x3, y3] = p3;

  const dx1 = x1 - x2, dx2 = x3 - x2, sx = x0 - x1 + x2 - x3;
  const dy1 = y1 - y2, dy2 = y3 - y2, sy = y0 - y1 + y2 - y3;

  const den = dx1 * dy2 - dx2 * dy1;
  const g = den === 0 ? 0 : (sx * dy2 - dx2 * sy) / den;
  const hh = den === 0 ? 0 : (dx1 * sy - sx * dy1) / den;

  const a = x1 - x0 + g * x1;
  const b = x3 - x0 + hh * x3;
  const c = x0;
  const d = y1 - y0 + g * y1;
  const e = y3 - y0 + hh * y3;
  const f = y0;

  // columns divided by the element's own dimensions
  const m = [
    a / w, d / w, 0, g / w,
    b / h, e / h, 0, hh / h,
    0, 0, 1, 0,
    c, f, 0, 1,
  ];
  return `matrix3d(${m.map((n) => Number(n.toFixed(6))).join(",")})`;
}

/** The quad in frame pixels, for a frame of the given size (plate covers it). */
export function screenQuadPx(frameW: number, frameH: number): [Pt, Pt, Pt, Pt] {
  // The plate is scaled to COVER the frame, then centred.
  const scale = Math.max(frameW / PLATE_W, frameH / PLATE_H);
  const drawnW = PLATE_W * scale, drawnH = PLATE_H * scale;
  const offX = (frameW - drawnW) / 2, offY = (frameH - drawnH) / 2;
  return SCREEN_QUAD.map(
    ([nx, ny]) => [offX + nx * drawnW, offY + ny * drawnH] as Pt,
  ) as [Pt, Pt, Pt, Pt];
}

  return { x: b.x + interpolate(back, [0, 1], [-GLANCE_X, 0]),

           zoom: interpolate(back, [0, 1], [BASE_ZOOM - 0.006, BASE_ZOOM]) };
}

/** Squarely on the screen, nothing panned off it. */
export function camNeutral(frameW: number, frameH: number) {
  const b = base(frameW, frameH);
  return { x: b.x, y: b.y, zoom: BASE_ZOOM };
}

/**
 * The review, R6-Q2. He sits back a touch and takes the whole page in.
 *
 * Pulling back rather than pushing in is the point: this is not another read,
 * it is a check that the whole thing is held. The eye covers everything, so the
 * camera has no one place to be.
 */
export function camReview(frameW: number, frameH: number, k: number) {
  const b = base(frameW, frameH);
  const out = Math.sin(Math.min(1, k) * Math.PI) * 0.024;
  return { x: b.x, y: b.y - 4 * Math.sin(k * Math.PI), zoom: BASE_ZOOM - out };
}

/** Where the eye is, as a pool of light travelling the words. */
export type ReadLight = {
  /** Chunk numbers being lit, or "all" during the review sweep. */
  ns: number[] | "all";
  /** 0..1 through the sweep. */
  p: number;
  /** Words covered by the pool. Wider for a check than for a read. */
  spread?: number;
};

/**
 * The stimulus as the drill renders it: everything readable, always.
 *
 * The only thing added is light. A word is never dimmed, never blurred and
 * never marked; the lit words simply have a warm pool sitting on them, the way
 * a lamp falls on a page. That is what keeps this from reading as an app
 * feature the student will go looking for and not find (Round 1).
 */
export const POVStimulus: React.FC<{
  chunks: Chunk[];
  light: ReadLight | null;
}> = ({ chunks, light }) => {
  // Words are laid out per chunk, but the pool travels the LIT RUN as one
  // stream, so it crosses a chunk boundary the way an eye does.
  const litNs: number[] =
    light === null ? [] :
    light.ns === "all" ? chunks.map((c) => c.n) : light.ns;

  const runWords: { cn: number; wi: number }[] = [];
  for (const c of chunks) {
    if (!litNs.includes(c.n)) continue;
    c.text.trim().split(/\s+/).forEach((_, wi) => runWords.push({ cn: c.n, wi }));
  }
  const centre = light && runWords.length ? light.p * (runWords.length - 1) : -1;
  const sigma = light?.spread ?? 1.7;

  const heatOf = (cn: number, wi: number): number => {
    if (centre < 0) return 0;
    const idx = runWords.findIndex((r) => r.cn === cn && r.wi === wi);
    if (idx < 0) return 0;
    const d = (idx - centre) / sigma;
    return Math.exp(-d * d);
  };

  return (
    <div
      style={{
        maxWidth: "52ch", color: SEC.ink, fontFamily: SEC.serif,
        fontSize: SEC.stimulusPx, fontWeight: 400, lineHeight: SEC.stimulusLh,
        fontOpticalSizing: "none", fontVariationSettings: '"opsz" 14',
      }}
    >
      {chunks.map((c) => (
        <span key={c.n}>
          {c.text.trim().split(/\s+/).map((w, wi) => {
            const h = heatOf(c.n, wi);
            const on = h > 0.02;
            return (
              <span
                key={wi}
                style={on ? {
                  background: `rgba(255,203,126,${(0.46 * h).toFixed(3)})`,
                  boxShadow: `0 0 ${(19 * h).toFixed(1)}px ${(10 * h).toFixed(1)}px rgba(255,200,118,${(0.36 * h).toFixed(3)})`,
                  borderRadius: 5,
                  color: h > 0.35 ? "#0a0f1c" : SEC.ink,...

import React from "react";
import { interpolate, Easing } from "remotion";
import { SEC } from "../rrc/tokens";
import { enterAt } from "./Float";

/*
 * The Recall, made visible.
 *
 * R6-Q3b / R6-Q4. The screen during The Recall is blank, and that is correct:
 * `SectionStage.tsx` shows a bare stimulus area with the footer "Say the whole
 * argument back from memory." The film was faithful to that and therefore ran
 * 23 seconds of blank white panel with a voice over it. He flagged it twice as
 * a missing step, because the part that gets scored looked like dead air.
 *
 * So the screen stays honestly blank and the argument rebuilds BESIDE it, one
 * sentence at a time, timed to `recall/generated/*.recall.timing.json`. Each
 * line lands as he says it and STAYS, so by the end the whole argument is
 * sitting in the room, visibly reassembled from memory next to a screen that
 * is showing nothing. That is the difference between hearing "every chunk
 * combined" and seeing it.
 *
 * Why this does not break the Round 1 rule that annotations must never read as
 * app UI: the rule is satisfied by GEOMETRY, not styling. These hang in
 * perspective with real thickness and a bloom on the surface behind them, in
 * front of the monitor rather than on it. Nothing in the real drill floats in
 * your room.
 */

export type RecallSentence = { index: number; text: string; start: number; end: number };

/** Lines shorter than this are beats, not content, and never get their own card. */
const MIN_WORDS = 3;

export const RecallStack: React.FC<{
  sentences: RecallSentence[];
  /** Seconds since the recall beat began. */
  t: number;
  fps: number;
}> = ({ sentences, t, fps }) => {
  const lines = sentences.filter((s) => s.text.trim().split(/\s+/).length >= MIN_WORDS);
  const shown = lines.filter((s) => t >= s.start);
  if (shown.length === 0) return null;

  /*
   * Everything said so far stays up, so the column has to fit the WHOLE argument
   * by the end. Size is therefore chosen from the full line count, never the
   * count so far, or the stack would visibly shrink under itself as it grew.
   *
   * Chosen by trying sizes rather than by a fudge factor: the number of lines a
   * sentence wraps to depends on the size, and the height depends on the lines,
   * so a single formula gets it wrong in both directions. Q1's third sentence is
   * 291 characters and was the case that broke the guess.
   */
  const AVAIL = 500;   // room-space px between the stack top and the page bottom
  const WIDTH = 1040;  // overhangs the monitor by a deliberate margin, not an accidental one
  let size = 30, gap = 11;
  for (; size > 17; size--) {
    const cpl = Math.max(20, Math.floor(WIDTH / (size * 0.52)));
    const h = lines.reduce(
      (a, s) => a + Math.ceil(s.text.length / cpl) * size * 1.34 + 18 + gap, 0);
    if (h <= AVAIL) break;
  }
  const fit = size / 30;

  return (
    <div
      style={{
        position: "absolute", left: 250, top: 290, width: WIDTH,
        perspective: 1150, perspectiveOrigin: "42% 46%", zIndex: 6,
      }}
    >
      <div
        style={{
          transformStyle: "preserve-3d",
          transform: "rotateX(4deg) rotateY(-7deg)",
          display: "flex", flexDirection: "column", gap: Math.round(gap * fit),
        }}
      >
        {shown.map((s) => {
          const since = Math.round((t - s.start) * fps);
          const e = enterAt(since, fps, 96);
          /* Older lines settle back in Z so the newest reads as the live one,
             while everything already said stays fully legible. */
          const age = shown.length - 1 - shown.indexOf(s);
          const back = Math.min(age, 5) * 5;
          const dim = interpolate(Math.min(age, 5), [0, 5], [1, 0.72]);
          return (
            <div
              key={s.index}
              style={{
                transform:
                  `translateZ(${(-bac...

70:## Round 6 (2026-09-10): the instructional cut, corrected against the app
71-
72-The film had drifted from the drill it teaches. Every change below traces to
73-`read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json` in the LSAT U repo or to a
74-decision captured in `brainstorms/2026-09-10-rrc-instructional-video-round6.md`.
75-
76-**The rule that would have prevented most of it:** never paraphrase a
77-student-facing string that already exists in the app. Go and read it. Two of the
78-four step labels were inventions ("Reading", "Colouring In"); the app says
79-"Read it." and "Colour it.", and its `briefing.sections[1].steps[].short` lines
80-are already second-person imperatives written in his voice.
81-
82-**Nothing hides.** `chunkBlur` is gone. The briefing is explicit that the whole
83-stimulus stays readable and that looking away is the student's own discipline:
84-"a screen that hides the text for you is a crutch that cannot come with you."
85-The old blur demonstrated exactly that crutch. Attention is now carried by the
86-camera glance plus a warm pool of light travelling the words at his real reading
87-pace - light ADDS emphasis without taking legibility away, which is why it is
88-compatible where blur was not.
89-
90-**Steps 2 and 3 are split at a measured boundary.** The old cut labelled the
91-0.4s breath between reading and speaking "Tell it back" and all 17-34s of speech
92-"Colouring In", so step 2 flashed on and off inside half a second. Step 2 is the
93-first part of what he SAYS. Boundaries live in `align/boundaries.json` as
94-phrases and resolve against `align/<slug>.words.json`, so the timing is measured
95-from his run. **Phrases must match the Whisper stream, not `walkthroughs.json`** -
96-the two differ ("Like if you have an infection" vs "Like, you have an infection").
97-
98-**The camera compensates for an off-centre plate.** `centringShift` in
99-`quad.ts`. The detected screen quad spans plate x 0.071-0.616, so the monitor's
100-centroid sits at x=0.34 and the camera never accounted for it. Panning right
101-pulls the plate's left edge toward frame, so how far you can pan is capped by
102-zoom: `maxShift = (drawnW * zoom - frameW) / 2`. Full centring needs zoom >= 1.45,
103-where the monitor stands 994px tall in a 1080 frame and the room disappears. At
104-1.26 the monitor centre moves 654 -> 825 of 1920 and the room survives. The
105-function CLAMPS and reports `shortfall` rather than silently under-delivering.
106-
107-**Audio edits.** `EDITS` in `align/build_script.py`, spec in `recall/patches.json`,
108-replacement voice from `recall/generate_patches.py` (same Chatterbox path as the
109-recall passages). Two traps:
110-
111-- **Measure every emitted piece, never assume its nominal length.** An ffmpeg
112-  `-ss/-to` slice lands on a frame boundary, so a slice is never exactly `b-a`.
113-  Assuming it put picture 0.22s off audio by the end of Q1, which splices most.
114-- **A cue is a caption line written against the UNEDITED run.** A cue straddling
115-  a cut still contains the removed words - Q1's "like a volcano situation. Who
116-  knows?" is one, and the first half is exactly what came out. Beats carry a
117-  piecewise `map` so lookups stay correct after a cut, and `build_script` emits
118-  `<slug>.cues.instr.json` with affected cues rewritten from the word stream.
119-  The instructional compositions must use those, never `<slug>.cues.json`.
120-
121-**Two things only a render will tell you.** Both of these type-checked, built
122-clean, and were wrong:
123-
124-- `stackLeave(2)` was passed a constant where the beat's `k` belonged. It clamps
125-  to 0, so the recall stack - the entire fix for "the recall is missing" - was
126-  invisible for its whole duration.
127-- A label at room y=92 lands in the monitor BEZEL, beside the app's own header
128-  and at much the same size, which reads as app chrome. Annotations belong over

Kling CLI - image/video generation, reusable Elements, and motion control (MCP client in CLI form)

Here's what you can do with Kling:

📷 Generate images
   · Text to image — "Draw me a cyberpunk-style cat"
   · Image to image — "Use this image as a reference and restyle it in watercolor"

🎬 Generate videos
   · Text to video — "Generate a 5-second video of a sunset over the sea"
   · Image to video — "Bring this image to life"

🧩 Reusable subjects — create and manage character, animal, prop, and other Elements
🎭 Motion control — drive a subject image with a motion video or saved motion

📤 Upload assets — local images work out of the box and are uploaded to Kling automatically

You can also combine agent and skill capabilities to build more complex creative workflows — like making an ad spot, a short story film, or batch-producing a set of assets.

Getting started (recommended order):
  1. kling login        Log in first (browser OAuth)
  2. kling tool_list    See which tools the server currently offers (login required)
  3. kling <command>    Run a specific command (e.g. text_to_image)

Usage:
  kling <command> [options]
  node --experimental-strip-types kling-cli/src/cli.ts <command> [options]

Commands (1:1 with backend MCP tools, plus client-side commands):
  who_am_i             Identity and tool capabilities (call first)
  text_to_image        Text-to-image generation
  image_to_image       Reference-image-to-image generation
  text_to_video        Text-to-video generation
  image_to_video       Image-to-video generation
  query_tasks          Query generation status by generation_id
  file_upload          Upload a file and return its URL
  element_create       Create a reusable Element subject
  element_list         List the current user's Elements
  element_get          Get full Element details
  element_update       Update selected Element fields
  element_delete       Delete an Element
  motion_library_list  List saved motion assets
  motion_control       Drive a subject image with a video or saved motion
  feedback             Report stuck tasks, billing anomalies, or unexpected results
  logout               Log out on the server and clear local credentials
  account              Account info (membership + credits)
  tool_list            List tools advertised by the MCP server (tools/list)
  login                Login and save credentials

Global flags:
  --omni       Use the omni model family (generation commands)
  --skill-name <n> / --skill-version <v>  Optional telemetry: identify the calling skill
  --quiet, -q  Compact single-line JSON output
  --help,  -h  Show help (top-level or per command)
  --version,-v Print CLI version

4. **Read-along light.** "should ... light up s the read so it akes itclear wherethey re eye are as the yread".
5. **Screen is off-centre.** "the screen is offcentred igt should mroe centred".

MCP tool: image_to_image

Description:
Generate new images or edit images from reference image(s) and a text prompt with Kling AI. Call who_am_i first to discover the available models, each model's argument specification, and its required inputs. Input URLs must either be links returned by the file_upload tool for local uploads, or publicly accessible external file URLs. Supported image specs: PNG/JPG only, below 4K resolution, ≤30MB, aspect ratio no wider than 1:2. URLs generated by prior Kling tasks can be used directly without downloading or validation. Every job is charged; do not submit trial jobs.- If user uses alias words, map to canonical names and pass canonical only. Do not invent names or value.- On timeout/long hang/failure/unexpected result, keep user informed. Ask user whether to retry or change parameters. Do not auto-resubmit or silently change intent/parameters. Image-subject binding: image_to_image CAN bind image subjects (cover + secondary, no resource.video) on every model that lists 'elements', INCLUDING kling-image-o1. const SCRIPTS: Record<string, Script> = {
  PT105_S2_Q1: s1 as Script,
  PT105_S2_Q2: s2 as Script,
  PT105_S2_Q3: s3 as Script,
};

/* R6-Q4: the sentence timings of the synthesised Recall, so the argument can
   rebuild in the room in step with his voice rather than on a guessed cadence. */
const RECALL: Record<string, RecallSentence[]> = {
  PT105_S2_Q1: r1.sentences as RecallSentence[],
  PT105_S2_Q2: r2.sentences as RecallSentence[],
  PT105_S2_Q3: r3.sentences as RecallSentence[],
};

export const RemotionRoot: React.FC = () => (
  <>
    <Composition
      id="RRCWalkthrough"
      component={RRC as never}
      durationInFrames={plan(payload.timeline, FPS).total}
      fps={FPS}
      width={1920}
      height={1080}
      defaultProps={payload as never}
    />

    {/* The POV cut, one composition per stimulus. Duration is always the real
        audio length from the aligned timeline, never a guessed number. */}
    {POV.map(({ id, slug, qIndex, scaffold }) => {
      const tl = (timeline as Payload["timeline"])[slug];
      return (
        <Composition
          key={id}
          id={id}
          component={POVWorking as never}
          durationInFrames={Math.round(tl.duration * FPS)}
          fps={FPS}
          width={1920}
          height={1080}
          defaultProps={{
            slug,
            chunks: (stimuli as Payload["stimuli"])[slug].chunks,
            segments: tl.segments as unknown as Seg[],
            cues: CUES[slug],
            audioSrc: `audio/${slug}.pov.mp3`,
            where: "PT105 Section 2",
            qIndex,
            qTotal: 23,
            scaffold,
          } as never}
        />
      );
    })}

    {POV.map(({ slug, qIndex }) => {
      const sc = SCRIPTS[slug];
      return (
        <Composition
          key={`INSTR${qIndex}`}
          id={`InstrQ${qIndex}`}
          component={POVInstructional as never}
          durationInFrames={Math.round(sc.duration * FPS)}
          fps={FPS}
          width={1920}
          height={1080}
          defaultProps={{
            slug,
            chunks: (stimuli as Payload["stimuli"])[slug].chunks,
            script: sc,
            cues: INSTR_CUES[slug],
            recall: RECALL[slug],
            audioSrc: `audio/${slug}.instr.mp3`,
            where: "PT105 Section 2",
            qIndex,
            qTotal: 23,
          } as never}
        />
      );
    })}
  </>
);
/**
 * Note: When using the Node.JS APIs, the config file
 * doesn't apply. Instead, pass options directly to the APIs.
 *
 * All configuration options: https://remotion.dev/docs/config
 */

import { Config } from "@remotion/cli/config";
import { enableTailwind } from '@remotion/tailwind-v4';

/*
 * rspack is OFF deliberately. With `Config.setRspack(true)` on this machine the
 * bundler reports 'Bundling 100%' and exits 0 but emits no JavaScript at all:
 * build/ gets index.html and favicon.ico and nothing else. Every downstream
 * command then dies with a misleading "ENOENT ... bundle.js", which is
 * Remotion's source-map reader failing, not the real fault.
 *
 * Webpack (the default) bundles correctly. Verified 2026-09-09 by rendering
 * MyComp to a real 60-frame MP4. Do not re-enable without re-testing a render.
 */
// Config.setRspack(true);
Config.setVideoImageFormat("jpeg");
Config.setOverwriteOutput(true);
Config.overrideBundlerConfig(enableTailwind);

<!doctype html>
<!--
  KALLAWAY · tier2 · lower-third · NAMEPLATE3D
  An extruded 3D glass nameplate rendered with CSS perspective tilt — the card
  leans back in space, revealing a thick glowing side-depth edge. The name sits
  on the face; role on a dimmer sub-ledge. Sits over live speaker footage.
  Slots: name (~26 chars), role (~40 chars).
-->
<html>
  <head>
    <meta charset="utf-8" />
    <link rel="stylesheet" href="../../tokens.css" />
    <style>
      html, body { margin: 0; background: transparent; }

      #t2-lt-np3d {
        position: absolute; inset: 0; width: 1920px; height: 1080px;
        background: transparent; overflow: hidden;
        font-family: var(--font-display);
      }

      /* Localized ambient bloom behind the card */
      #t2-lt-np3d .local-bloom {
        position: absolute;
        left: 40px; bottom: 80px;
        width: 600px; height: 260px;
        border-radius: 50%;
        background: radial-gradient(ellipse at 40% 80%,
          rgba(var(--accent-rgb), 0.30) 0%,
          rgba(var(--accent-rgb), 0.10) 48%,
          transparent 75%);
        filter: blur(40px);
        pointer-events: none;
        z-index: 1;
      }

      /* Perspective stage */
      #t2-lt-np3d .stage {
        position: absolute;
        left: 80px; bottom: 130px;
        perspective: 900px;
        perspective-origin: 30% 60%;
        z-index: 2;
      }

      /* The 3D-tilted plate */
      #t2-lt-np3d .plate-3d {
        position: relative;
        width: 500px;
        transform-style: preserve-3d;
        transform: rotateX(10deg) rotateY(-7deg);
      }

      /* Face of the nameplate */
      #t2-lt-np3d .face {
        position: relative;
        padding: 30px 40px 26px;
        background: var(--glass-bg-dark);
        backdrop-filter: var(--glass-blur);
        -webkit-backdrop-filter: var(--glass-blur);
        border-radius: var(--radius);
        border: var(--stroke) solid var(--glass-border);
        box-shadow:
          var(--glass-shadow),
          0 0 0 1.5px rgba(var(--accent-rgb), 0.55),
          0 0 28px rgba(var(--accent-rgb), 0.25),
          inset 0 1px 0 var(--glass-hi);
        overflow: hidden;
      }

      /* Top hairline shimmer */
      #t2-lt-np3d .face::before {
        content: "";
        position: absolute; top: 0; left: 10px; right: 10px; height: 1px;
        background: linear-gradient(90deg, transparent, rgba(255,255,255,0.60) 40%,
                                     rgba(255,255,255,0.60) 60%, transparent);
      }

      /* Simulated left depth-edge (the "extrusion") */
      #t2-lt-np3d .depth-edge {
        position: absolute;
        left: -10px; top: 6px; bottom: -6px;
        width: 10px;
        border-radius: 4px 0 0 4px;
        background: linear-gradient(180deg,
          rgba(var(--accent-rgb), 0.60) 0%,
          rgba(var(--accent-rgb), 0.28) 50%,
          rgba(var(--accent-rgb), 0.08) 100%);
        box-shadow: -4px 0 18px rgba(var(--accent-rgb), 0.45);
      }

      /* Bottom depth-edge */
      #t2-lt-np3d .depth-bottom {
        position: absolute;
        bottom: -7px; left: 6px; right: -4px;

        border-radius: 0 0 4px 4px;
        background: linear-gradient(90deg,

          rgba(var(--accent-rgb), 0.10) 70%,
          transparent 100%);
        box-shadow: 0 4px 16px rgba(var(--accent-rgb), 0.28);
      }

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_result]
## KLING - LOGGED IN AND CAPABILITIES VERIFIED (2026-09-09)
`kling login` completed via browser OAuth. Tokens at `C:\Users\Joshu\.kling\.credentials`.
Persistent - does not need repeating.

### Account
- userId 105315947, membership **VIP / Standard**, **660 credits available**.
- His "six credits a day" estimate was wrong. Budget is real.

### Resolution - 4K IS AVAILABLE (my earlier hedge was wrong, in his favour)
- `kling-video-v3_0` and `kling-video-v3_0_omni`: resolution **['720p','1080p','4k']**,
  and **4k is the DEFAULT**. Both text_to_video and image_to_video.
- `v3_0_turbo`, `v2_5`, `v2_6`, `o1`: 1080p ceiling.
- motion_control: 720p/1080p only.

### Duration
- v3_0 / v3_0_omni / v3_0_turbo: **3-15 seconds** (not the 5s I assumed).
- v2_5 / v2_6: 5 or 10 only. o1 image_to_video: 3-10.

### Aspect
- 16:9 supported natively on text_to_video across the v3 family. Matches the
  in-app 16:9 placement from R2-Q5.

### Image models available for seed stills
kling-image-v3_0 / v3_0_omni / o1 / v2_1, plus gemini-3-pro-image,
gemini-3.1-flash-image, gpt-image-2. 16:9 available on all.

### Chosen generation pipeline (decision)
1. `text_to_image` at 16:9 to author each POV still - cheap, controllable,
   and it fixes composition before any video credit is spent.
2. `image_to_video` with **kling-video-v3_0 at 4k**, duration chosen per plate,
   to add the camera move. Seeding from a locked still is what keeps the room
   identical across plates, which matters because plates are reused (R3-Q11).
3. `query_tasks` to poll, then pull `works[].url`.

### Other CLI facts
- `kling account` gives membership + credits. `kling element_*` creates reusable
  subjects. `motion_control` drives a subject image with a motion video.
- Global `--omni` flag selects the omni family. `--quiet` gives single-line JSON.

## Cross-session handoff received 2026-09-09
A parallel Claude session relayed its state (Joshua confirmed he had two sessions
running on this same task). Everything it reported matches what is already in
- `src/pov/Room.tsx` - plate, camera, monitor composite, optical defocus.
- `src/pov/Float.tsx` - Plate3D / SpeechBubble / CheckLine, kit techniques.
- `src/pov/POVWorking.tsx` - one stimulus in the room.
- `align/treat_audio.py` - the two-layer audio treatment.
- `recall/generate_recall.py` - cloned Recall generation.

### The room
`public/plates/room-v3.png`, 5504x3072, from `gemini-3-pro-image` at 16:9 / 4k.
Three variants generated; v3 chosen for the largest screen area (55% of frame
width), the study context, and the window placed right for the glance-off.

### Screen quad, and the two defects found by rendering
Detected by masking the light low-saturation region:
  TL 0.0712,0.1198  TR 0.6156,0.1839  BR 0.6052,0.7266  BL 0.0752,0.7500
- **Defect 1: composite undersized.** First render showed a grey wedge of the
  original plate down the right edge, widening toward the bottom. The mask stops
  inside the bezel because the screen vignettes at its border. A second detection
  by line-fitting DISAGREED with the first on the bottom edge, so both undershoot.
  Fixed with `OVERSCAN = 1.042`, pushing the quad out from its centroid so the
  composite buries its edge under the bezel. Verified by cropping the edge at
  high magnification: wedge gone.
- **Defect 2: plate edges exposed.** BASE_ZOOM 1.075 was not enough for a 232px
  glance; black bars appeared. The wrapper scales about its centre, so the safe
  translate is (zoom-1)/2*1920. Raised to 1.30. Costs nothing: the plate is
  5504px feeding a 1920px frame.
- Residual: a very thin light line still shows at some camera positions. Minor,
  not fixed, flagged to him rather than hidden.

### Audio treatment, verified
`align/treat_audio.py` splits on the real read spans from timeline.json.
Interior: highpass 95, lowpass 2350, +3.2dB at 220Hz, -4dB at 3kHz, volume 0.72.
Fix: anchors must satisfy `frameX = 960 + (roomX - 960) * 1.30 < ~1900`.
  SpeechBubble  x 1268 -> 1120, w 620 -> 560...
[/external_agent_tool_result]

s=open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()
plan='''
## Build plan (2026-09-10, written after scouting, before any code)

All of this is my call under the Q4 licence. Recorded so the reasoning is traceable and so the next session does not re-derive it.

### Facts that shaped it (verified 2026-09-10)
- `room-v3.png` is 5504x3072, generated by gemini-3-pro-image via Kling. Monitor spans plate x 0.071-0.616. Window and curtain are on the RIGHT, bookshelves left and behind. Desk has a notebook and a water glass on the right, in front of the window.
- Kling: 641 credits. Image models include gemini-3-pro-image (Nano Banana Pro) image_to_image at 4k, aspect up to 21:9, image_count up to 9. kling-video-v3_0 image_to_video at 4k, 3-15s, `prefer_multi_shots` MUST be false.
- Higgsfield: 0 credits, free plan. Its outpaint/upscale tools are unavailable. Not buying.
- Machine: i5-1334U, Intel UHD integrated graphics, 16 GB, 272 GB free. No CUDA. Chatterbox venv has torch-cpu 2.6, transformers 5.2, numpy, scipy, PIL. No opencv.
- Remotion 4.0.523 with `--scale` flag: 4K = render the 1920x1080 compositions at scale 2. `--jpeg-quality`, `--crf` available.
- The beat scripts interleave `card` beats before read/tell/colour in Q1 (0-5s card read, 5-8.3 read, 8.3-13.7 card tell, 13.7-19 tell, 19-24.6 card colour, 24.6-33.5 colour, ...). Q2/Q3 have no cards. Seven retells in total (Q1 x3, Q2 x2, Q3 x2).

### The head turn: how it is built
1. **Panorama, not a wider flat photo.** A flat outpaint cannot represent a 44-degree turn: the far edge of a planar image at 70 degrees is stretched 3.7x. The plate is mapped onto a cylinder at its own field of view (estimated ~65 deg horizontal). The room to the RIGHT (window wall, beyond the curtain) is generated as a second *view*, camera rotated ~45 deg right, by gemini-3-pro-image image_to_image with room-v3 as the reference. A 21:9 outpaint is generated too, as glue for the 31-45 deg overlap. All three are projected onto the cylinder and feather-blended in angular order: plate (untouched pixels, full res) -> outpaint wing -> rotated view. Backup if the still views are incoherent: kling-video-v3_0 image_to_video "camera pans right", frames stitched into the cylinder.
2. **Facing view is the same plate, centred.** Frame hFOV ~53 deg (matches the monitor size zoom 1.26 gave), yaw 0 aimed at the monitor centre. No left wing is needed because the frame is narrower than the plate. This is the Q3 "screen centred, straight in front" requirement, achieved without remaking the room.
3. **Turned view.** Yaw ~44 deg right, pitch -4, roll +1.5 (a head, not a tripod). The monitor's right edge sits ~2 deg inside the frame's left edge: a sliver of bezel, no text (Q2). Frame right edge reaches ~70 deg, all generated room.
4. **Renderer: Python (numpy/scipy), offline.** Rotation-only reprojection of a cylindrical panorama to a pinhole view is a per-pixel lookup; 4K frames in ~1s each on CPU. No WebGL in headless Chrome, no new npm dependencies, deterministic, debuggable as PNGs. The camera model lives in TypeScript (`src/pov/headTurn.ts`) and is EXPORTED per frame as JSON; Python consumes that JSON. One model, two consumers, verified by projecting the monitor corners in both and comparing.
5. **Only the moving frames are pre-rendered:** turn-away (~0.9s), turn-back (~0.8s), half-turn for card beats (~20 deg, both directions), plus the facing still and the turned still. Motion blur baked by averaging 4 sub-frame samples. Reused for all seven retells (Q4). The 20s+ holds use the stills with the existing 2D living drift.
6. **Parallax (phase 2, if the base works):** Depth Anything V2 on CPU gives a depth map for the panorama; the eye is offset ~6 cm during the turn because a head rotates about the neck, not the eye. That is the "3D effect" the flat still cannot give. Implemented as depth-guided inverse mapping in the same Python renderer.
7. **DOM overlays are world-anchored.** Every card, bubble, label and the "Look Away" text is a planar rectangle in room coordinates (cylinder angle, height, distance). Its four corners are projected through the same camera and applied with the existing `quadTransform` matrix3d. So the overlays rotate with the head exactly as the room does, and the monitor's Screen composite uses the identical path.

### Effects (his "coolest effects possible", each tied to something real)
- Gaze-shift blink at the start of the turn-away only (people blink when they shift gaze). Three frames, soft, masks the onset. Never on the return.
- Motion blur (baked, 4 samples), stronger at the fastest part of the turn.
- Peripheral softness: a blurred copy of the plate under a radial mask, strongest at the frame edge on the monitor side while turned. This is the Q3 "blurred to the side / corner of the eye" permission, and it is optical, not a mask on text.
- Exposure adaptation: brightness lifts as the window enters, then settles over ~0.6s. Turning toward a bright window is what a real eye does.
- Lens breathing: hFOV widens ~2 deg during the turn (focus relaxes), tightens on return.
- Ease profile: fast start, long settle with a 2% overshoot; return is quicker and settles square.
- Living drift on holds stays (existing), now in yaw/pitch terms so the overlays move with the room.

### "Look Away" and the bubbles
- "Look Away" is world-anchored in the free space in front of the curtain, large, Instrument Serif italic (HyperFrames type roster), chrome gradient, layered extrusion, local bloom. Appears once the turn passes ~60%, word-revealed, and leaves naturally when the head returns. His wording, verbatim.
- Speech bubbles (tell/colour) live in the free space under "Look Away", never on the monitor. Existing cue text (already short lines). Bubbly: rounder glass, tail toward the viewer, spring in.
- Step label + one-line cue while facing: a slim glass placard standing on the DESK, lower right, world-anchored on the desk plane. Off the passage entirely.
- Step intro cards (Q1 only): the head half-turns ~20 deg right and the card stands in the room beside the monitor. The dark full-frame dim is gone; the passage stays visible and unobstructed. This is the fix for "bubble in the way of the stimulus".
- The Recall: head stays turned; the rebuilt argument stacks in the free space beneath "Look Away"; the count sits under it. Head returns for the Space presses.

### Highlight ("more in touch")
The reading light becomes a highlighter that hugs the words: per-word marker fill with a slightly irregular edge (SVG turbulence), advancing word by word at his measured reading pace, with a much tighter glow. Same for the review sweep. Nothing is dimmed or hidden.

### 4K and delivery
Render with `--scale 2` (3840x2160) from the 1920-logic compositions. Pre-rendered turn frames are produced at 3840x2160 so they are native at that scale. Deliver `out/r7/instr-q{1,2,3}-4k.mp4` plus 1080p copies.

### Credit cap
My own cap: 150 Kling credits for this round before checking in. Every generation is logged below with its cost.

### Verification gates (in order)
1. Camera model: Python and TS project the monitor corners to the same pixels (< 1 px at 4K).
2. A facing still and a turned still, sent to him before anything moves.
3. A 6-second turn-away/hold/turn-back test clip.
4. Q1 at 1080p, all beats, for the seam and timing check.
5. Q1/Q2/Q3 at 4K.
'''
s=s.replace('\n## Open flags (pending input)',plan+'\n## Open flags (pending input)')
open(p,'w',encoding='utf-8').write(s)
EOF
grep -c "Build plan" brainstorms/2026-09-10-rrc-instructional-video-round7.md
[/external_agent_tool_call]

description: Test Depth Anything V2 on CPU against the plate
command: S=C:/Users/Joshu/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/6bf8aeea-2d39-4a6c-aae3-8d213ed8c968/scratchpad && cat > $S/depth_test.py <<'EOF'
import time, sys, numpy as np
from PIL import Image
t0=time.time()
from transformers import pipeline
pipe = pipeline(task="depth-estimation", model="depth-anything/Depth-Anything-V2-Small-hf")
print("load", round(time.time()-t0,1), flush=True)
im = Image.open(r"C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\video\public\plates\room-v3.png").convert("RGB")
im2 = im.resize((1376, 768))
t1=time.time()
out = pipe(im2)
d = out["predicted_depth"]
try: d = d.numpy()
except Exception: d = np.array(d)
print("infer", round(time.time()-t1,1), d.shape, float(d.min()), float(d.max()), flush=True)
d = (d - d.min())/(d.max()-d.min()+1e-9)
Image.fromarray((d*255).astype(np.uint8)).resize(im.size, Image.BILINEAR).save(sys.argv[1])
print("saved", flush=True)
EOF
cd $S && C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/hyperframes-student-kit/.venv/Scripts/python.exe depth_test.py $S/depth_small.png 2>&1 | grep -v Warning | tail -8
[/external_agent_tool_call]

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Fix plate camera centring and tsconfig, re-export
command: cd C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/video && python - <<'EOF'
p='src/pov/headTurn.ts'; s=open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()
old='''export function plateCam(): Cam {
  const f = focalPx(PLATE_W, PLATE_HFOV);
  const cx = PLATE_QUAD.reduce((a, p) => a + p[0], 0) / 4;
  const cy = PLATE_QUAD.reduce((a, p) => a + p[1], 0) / 4;
  const yaw = -Math.atan(((cx - 0.5) * PLATE_W) / f) / DEG;
  const pitch = Math.atan(((cy - 0.5) * PLATE_H) / f) / DEG;
  return { yaw, pitch, roll: 0, hfov: PLATE_HFOV, eye: [0, 0, 0] };
}'''
new='''export function plateCam(): Cam {
  const f = focalPx(PLATE_W, PLATE_HFOV);
  // "Centred" means the MEAN of the four corner directions sits at theta 0,
  // phi 0. The mean of the pixel corners is not the same thing in perspective.
  const th = PLATE_QUAD.map(([u]) => Math.atan(((u - 0.5) * PLATE_W) / f));
  const ph = PLATE_QUAD.map(([, v]) => Math.atan(((v - 0.5) * PLATE_H) / f));
  const yaw = -(th.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) / 4) / DEG;
  // y is down, and positive pitch looks down, so a monitor ABOVE the plate's
  // axis (v < 0.5) needs the plate camera pitched UP by that much: negative.
  const pitch = (ph.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) / 4) / DEG;
  return { yaw, pitch, roll: 0, hfov: PLATE_HFOV, eye: [0, 0, 0] };
}'''
assert old in s; s=s.replace(old,new); open(p,'w',encoding='utf-8').write(s)
t='tsconfig.json'; c=open(t).read()
c=c.replace('"noEmit": true,','"noEmit": true,\n    "allowImportingTsExtensions": true,')
open(t,'w').write(c)
EOF
node align/export_camera.ts 2>&1 | tail -4 && npx tsc --noEmit; echo tsc $?
[/external_agent_tool_call]

description: Fix fill and seam placement, rebuild the panorama
command: cd C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/video && python - <<'EOF'
p='align/pano/build_pano.py'; s=open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()
old_start=s.index('def fill_extend(img, empty):')
old_end=s.index('if __name__ == "__main__":')
new='''def fill_extend(img, empty):
    """Every uncovered pixel takes the nearest covered pixel (2-D, via distance transform)."""
    H, W = empty.shape
    dist, labels = cv2.distanceTransformWithLabels(empty.astype(np.uint8), cv2.DIST_L2, 5, labelType=cv2.DIST_LABEL_PIXEL)
    # labels index the zero (covered) pixels in raster order of the label map
    ys, xs = np.nonzero(~empty)
    order = np.zeros(labels.max() + 1, np.int64)
    lab_cov = labels[ys, xs]
    order[lab_cov] = ys * W + xs
    src = order[labels]
    flat = img.reshape(-1, 3)
    out = flat[src.ravel()].reshape(H, W, 3)
    return np.where(empty[..., None], out, img)

'''
s=s[:old_start]+new+s[old_end:]
s=s.replace('''            owner[y0:y1] = np.where(w > owner_w(owner[y0:y1], i, w), i + 1, owner[y0:y1]) if False else np.where((w > 0.5), i + 1, owner[y0:y1])''','''            owner[y0:y1] = np.where((w > 0.5), i + 1, owner[y0:y1])''')
s=s.replace('''def owner_w(o, i, w):
    return 0

''','')
open(p,'w',encoding='utf-8').write(s)
m='align/pano/make_config.py'; t=open(m,encoding='utf-8').read()
t=t.replace('''    sources = [
        {"name": "plate", "path": "public/plates/room-v3.png", "hfov": P["hfov"], "yaw": pc["yaw"], "pitch": pc["pitch"], "roll": 0,
         "theta_use": [-1e9, th_m], "feather": a.seam_feather},
        {"name": "rot45-3", "path": a.rot, "hfov": hfov_r, "yaw": yaw_r, "pitch": pitch_r, "roll": 0,
         "theta_use": [th_m, 1e9], "feather": a.seam_feather},
    ]''','''    # The seam sits on the LEFT half of the mullion (plain white in both), so
    # the plate's weight reaches zero before its water glass begins at ~39.2 deg
    # and the glass appears exactly once, from the rotated view.
    seam = th_m - 1.2
    sources = [
        {"name": "plate", "path": "public/plates/room-v3.png", "hfov": P["hfov"], "yaw": pc["yaw"], "pitch": pc["pitch"], "roll": 0,
         "theta_use": [-1e9, seam], "feather": a.seam_feather},
        {"name": "rot45-3", "path": a.rot, "hfov": hfov_r, "yaw": yaw_r, "pitch": pitch_r, "roll": 0,
         "theta_use": [seam, 1e9], "feather": a.seam_feather},
    ]''')
t=t.replace('ap.add_argument("--seam-feather", type=float, default=1.6)','ap.add_argument("--seam-feather", type=float, default=1.1)')
t=t.replace('"out": "public/plates/r7/pano", "seam_theta": th_m}','"out": "public/plates/r7/pano", "seam_theta": seam}')
open(m,'w',encoding='utf-8').write(t)
EOF

description: Fix the turn rule for turned runs and check fonts available
command: cd C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/video && ls node_modules/@remotion/google-fonts/ | grep -i "InstrumentSerif\|Fraunces\|Inter.js" | head && python - <<'EOF'
p='src/pov/headState.ts'; s=open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()
a=s.index('export function headEvents'); b=s.index('export function poseAt')
new='''export function headEvents(beats: BeatLike[]): Ev[] {
  const ev: Ev[] = [];
  const turnedKind = (k?: string) => k === "tell" || k === "colour" || k === "recall";
  for (let i = 0; i < beats.length; i++) {
    const b = beats[i];
    const prev = beats[i - 1];
    const next = beats[i + 1];
    if (b.kind === "card") {
      ev.push({ t0: b.start, move: "halfAway" });
      ev.push({ t0: Math.max(b.start + dur("halfAway"), b.end - dur("halfBack")), move: "halfBack" });
    } else if (turnedKind(b.kind)) {
      // Entering a turned run: look away before the first word when coming off
      // a read; on the word when a card has only just returned the head.
      if (!turnedKind(prev?.kind)) {
        const lead = prev?.kind === "read" ? LEAD : 0;
        ev.push({ t0: b.start - lead, move: "away" });
      }
      // Leaving it: land on the page exactly as the next beat starts.
      if (!turnedKind(next?.kind)) {
        const endAt = next ? next.start : b.end;
        ev.push({ t0: endAt - dur("back"), move: "back" });
      }
    }
  }
  ev.sort((a, b) => a.t0 - b.t0);
  return ev;
}

'''
s=s[:a]+new+s[b:]
open(p,'w',encoding='utf-8').write(s)
EOF

        opacity: interpolate(e, [0, 0.45], [0, 1], { extrapolateRight: "clamp" }),

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Document Round 7 in the README
command: cd C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/video && cat >> README.md <<'EOF'

## Round 7 (2026-09-10): seen through the eyes

The camera is now a HEAD. It faces the page to read, turns ~40 degrees right
to tell it back (monitor reduced to a bezel sliver at the frame edge), and
comes back to check. Decisions in
`brainstorms/2026-09-10-rrc-instructional-video-round7.md`.

**Pipeline (run in this order after any change to `src/pov/headTurn.ts`):**

```bash
node align/export_camera.ts                                  # camera.json from the TS model
python align/pano/make_config.py align/pano/camera.json align/pano/config.json \
  --left public/plates/r7/outpaint-2.png --left-hfov 85.55 --left-yaw 8.90 --left-pitch 3.06 --left-roll 1.13 --left-until -21
python align/pano/build_pano.py align/pano/config.json       # ~2 min, pano.jpg 10753x4286
python align/pano/depth_pano.py public/plates/r7/pano.json   # Depth Anything V2 Base, CPU
python align/pano/render_views.py align/pano/camera.json public/plates/r7/pano.json public/plates/r7/seq --depth public/plates/r7/pano-depth.npy
npx remotion bundle && npx remotion render build InstrQ1 out/r7/instr-q1-4k.mp4 --scale 2
```
(python = the Chatterbox venv: `projects/hyperframes-student-kit/.venv/Scripts/python.exe`.)

**One camera model, two consumers.** `headTurn.ts` defines the kinematics and
the projection; Python only reads its JSON export. `render_views.py` projects
the monitor corners itself and refuses to render if it disagrees with the TS
numbers by more than 1 px (measured: 0.0000 px). Every overlay is a rectangle
IN THE ROOM (`World.tsx`, `anchors.ts`), projected through the same camera, so
nothing can sit on the page and everything turns with the head.

**Why a cylinder, not a wider photo.** A flat outpaint cannot hold a 40-degree
turn: the far edge of a planar image at 70 degrees is stretched 3.7x. The plate
and a second generated view (camera rotated ~45 degrees) are mapped onto a
cylinder and feather-blended on the door's plain white mullion.

**Traps found this round:**
- Nano Banana Pro will NOT preserve pixels when asked to outpaint; it
  re-renders the scene. Both 21:9 outpaints were unusable as glue (their left
  bookshelf strip was salvaged as a peripheral wing). 35 credits per 4k image.
- SIFT gives ~400 keypoints on a smooth generated interior; the pair could not
  be matched automatically. Three hand-read features on 100px-gridded crops
  placed the second view; the seam is invisible.
- Chrome paints `text-shadow` OVER a `background-clip: text` fill. The chrome
  face and the extrusion must be two stacked copies of the text.
- The head must not snap between poses: `headState.ts` treats tell/colour/recall
  as one turned RUN and only turns at its edges. Q1's colour card sits inside
  that run, so the head returns for the card and turns away again after it.
- `distanceTransformWithLabels` is the right fill for uncovered panorama
  corners; a row-wise extension leaves black bands where no valid pixel exists
  above.
EOF
echo ok
[/external_agent_tool_call]

**What changed since Round 6, all from your grill answers:**

The head faces the centred screen to read, turns 40° right to tell it back, and comes back. Neck parallax, shutter blur, a gaze-shift blink, and exposure adaptation toward the window are all in.
- **Screen centred.** Facing view has the monitor dead centre with bookshelf and curtain either side.
- **Nothing on the page.** Every card, bubble, and the Recall are objects in the room, projected through the same camera as the plate. Step cards stand beside the monitor during a half turn.
- **"Look Away"** is a chrome serif sign hanging in front of the curtain, your wording.
- **Tighter highlight.** A marker stroke on the words, not a glow over the line.

**One thing to flag:** Kling charged 35 credits per 4K image, so the five reference generations cost 175 credits, over the 150 cap I set myself. 466 remain. Two of the five were unusable as intended (the outpaints re-render the room), though one gave the bookshelf strip on the left.

**Not verified by anyone's eye yet:** whether 40° reads as "not too dramatic", whether the bezel sliver at the frame edge should stay or go, and whether the blink and exposure lift feel natural rather than like effects. Those are your calls once you've watched it.

  const fy = billboard ? anchor.theta + faceYaw : faceYaw;
  const ft = billboard ? -anchor.phi + faceTilt : faceTilt;
  const corners = rectAnchors(anchor, w, h, fy, ft);''')

import type React from "react";
import { ArrowRight } from "lucide-react";
import type { ActivityInstructions } from "../api/types";
import { renderInlineMarks } from "../prompt-format";
import "./section-launch.css";

export interface SectionBriefingPageProps {
  title: string;
  subtitle: string | null;
  instructions: ActivityInstructions;
  onStart: () => void;
  onExit?: () => void;
}

/**
 * The instructions, on their own page, before anything records.
 *
 * Pressing Start on a section used to open the microphone straight away, and
 * the method was folded into tabs on a page she had already scrolled past. This
 * page is the whole briefing laid out in order, every step numbered and open,
 * with one Start button at the top and the same one at the foot. The written
 * instructions sit under it, closed. Nothing here begins the run.
 */
export function SectionBriefingPage({
  title,
  subtitle,
  instructions,
  onStart,
  onExit,
}: SectionBriefingPageProps) {
  const briefing = instructions.briefing;
  const steps = briefing?.sections.find((s) => (s.steps?.length ?? 0) > 0)?.steps ?? [];

  const startButton = (label: string) => (
    <button type="button" className="sl-start" onClick={onStart}>
      {label}
      <ArrowRight size={16} strokeWidth={2.4} aria-hidden="true" />
    </button>
  );

  return (
    <div className="sl sl-briefing-page">
      <header className="sl-bp-head">
        <div>
          {/* No chip. The title already names the drill, and on the door the
              chip earns its place because the H1 there is the bare name. */}
          <h1 className="sl-title">{title}</h1>
          {(instructions.tagline || subtitle) && (
            <p className="sl-tagline">
              {instructions.tagline ? renderInlineMarks(instructions.tagline, "sl-bp-tag") : subtitle}
            </p>
          )}
        </div>
        <div className="sl-cta">
          {startButton("Start the section")}

[external_agent_tool_result]
# Copy corrections

Every student-facing wording fix, with the reason it was wrong. Read this before
writing or editing copy for LSAT U so the same mistake is not made twice.

Companion to [[teaching-voice]], which says how the writing should sound. This
says what has actually gone wrong and why.

Started 2026-09-07 from a Read, Recall, Colour pass. Add to it every time a
correction is made.

## The rules these corrections came from

Three rules already existed and all three leaked anyway, because each was
enforced on the components that happened to exist when it was written. A surface
added later starts uncovered.

- **No em dashes, ever.** [[teaching-voice]], Core voice.
- **No internal vocabulary in student copy.** `projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/CONTEXT.md`
  carries the glossary and marks what to avoid.
- **Every CSS token a stylesheet uses must be defined in it.** `projects/lsatu/CLAUDE.md`.

Guards now live with the content rather than with each component:
`content/instructions-voice.test.ts` checks the authored instructions, so every
surface that renders them is covered, including surfaces not written yet.

## Corrections

### "Recall Rephrase Colour" → "Read, Recall, Colour"
The door's heading was hardcoded to a name the product does not have, one line
under a chip reading "Read, Recall, Colour". "Rephrase" belongs to a different
Activity, Conditional Rephrase. A test asserted the wrong name, which is why
2,600 passing tests never caught it.
**Lesson:** when a test pins a string, check the string is right, not just that
it matches.

### "Intake only. Nothing comes back out yet…" → plain instruction
Clinical and abstract, and the sentence then told her to read "with full
intonation" while step one is silent reading. The clause that made intonation
internal, "hear it naturally in your head", had been dropped in an earlier edit.
**Lesson:** a word like "intake" reads as jargon. And when copy is trimmed,
check what the trimmed clause was load-bearing for.

### "Three beats through the stimulus" → "Three steps"
"Beat" is the internal code name for one of the four method steps. The glossary
says students see **step**. The interface ten pixels below labelled the same four
things Step 1 to Step 4, so the page used two names for one thing.
**Lesson:** never let an implementation name reach the page.

### "22 pieces to read" → line removed
A **piece** is one chunk, roughly a sentence, and the instructions on that same
page use the word that way. The count was of stimuli, so "22 pieces" read as 22
sentences.
**Lesson:** a unit word must mean one thing per surface. Check what a word
already means on the page before reusing it.

### "say it back" → "tell it back", "The Read" → "The Recall"
The library door still carried both retired names. "The Read" was step four's
former name and collided with step one, which is why it was renamed.
**Lesson:** a rename is not finished until every row carries it. The door was

# Teaching Voice

How LSAT U sounds, across every surface. This is the canonical voice doc: route all student-facing and brand copy through it. Built from a grill with Joshua (2026-06-13), then tested and enriched against the full corpus of his real correspondence (944 messages he authored, mid-2025 to mid-2026, from the Gmail takeout; 2026-07-18). Claims below marked with a quote are evidenced by that corpus.

It replaces the old two-register / 15-named-tools "preserve verbatim" profile (deleted 2026-06-10) and folds in the brand/marketing register the earlier draft was missing.

## Core voice (every register)

The constant under all three registers below. This is Joshua on the page.

- **Quiet authority.** State the value and let it land. Never oversell, never beg, never perform enthusiasm.
- **Specific, never generic.** Real sc...

import { render, screen } from "@testing-library/react";
import { describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import { SectionBriefingPage } from "./SectionBriefingPage";
import type { ActivityInstructions } from "../api/types";
import sectionInstructions from "../content/read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json";

/**
 * The instructions page, and the three ways it went wrong.
 *
 * It arrived without any of the guards the four older surfaces carry, which is
 * exactly how an em dash and a miscounted unit reached a student.
 */
function renderPage(props?: Partial<Parameters<typeof SectionBriefingPage>[0]>) {
  return render(
    <SectionBriefingPage
      title="Read, Recall, Colour · PT1 Section 3"
      subtitle="A whole section, out loud, against the clock"
      instructions={sectionInstructions as ActivityInstructions}
      onStart={vi.fn()}
      {...props}
    />,
  );
}

describe("the section briefing page", () => {
  it("says at the foot, beside Start, what one take and a pause each cost her, and what the time is for", () => {
    renderPage();
    const foot = document.querySelector(".sl-bp-foot")?.textContent ?? "";
    expect(foot).toMatch(/one take/i);
    // The runner ships a Pause button, so the foot says what pausing does
    // rather than denying it. Joshua's call, 2026-09-09.
    expect(foot).toMatch(/you can pause/i);
    expect(foot).toMatch(/the clock stops/i);
    expect(foot).toMatch(/cannot go back/i);
    expect(foot).toMatch(/goal/i);
    // The authored closer is the last beat before Start, and it renders here.
    expect(foot).toContain("One a day holds you steady.");
  });

  it("carries no em dash, which Joshua never types", () => {
    renderPage();
    expect(document.body.textContent ?? "").not.toMatch(/[—–]/);
  });

  it("carries no meta line: the title names the section and nothing else belongs there", () => {
    renderPage();
    expect(document.querySelector(".sl-bp-meta")).toBeNull();
    const text = document.body.textContent ?? "";
    expect(text).not.toMatch(/\d+ stimuli/);
    expect(text).not.toMatch(/about \d+ min/);
    // The count and the clock were the whole line, and both were noise here.
    // The clock is stated where it matters, at the foot and in the long version.
    expect(text).not.toContain("pieces to read");
  });

  it("keeps the internal writing vocabulary off the page", () => {
    renderPage();
    // CONTEXT.md: "Beat" is the internal code term for one of the four method
    // steps. Students see "step". The door already forbids these words, but the
    // door never renders the written instructions and this page does, so

/**
 * The house rules for student-facing copy, checked against the content itself.

 * These rules already existed. They were enforced per component, so each new

 * and a title the product does not have. The door's own guard could not have
 * caught the second one even in principle, because the door never renders the

 *
 * So the guard belongs to the words, not to whichever component draws them.
 * Every surface that renders this file is covered by this test, including the
 * ones not written yet.
 *
 * `coach` is deliberately exempt: it is the brief handed to the grader, not
 * copy a student reads, and it needs the precise internal vocabulary.
 */

/** Internal names, from the glossary in `../CONTEXT.md`. */

/** Retired names the product has moved off. */
const RETIRED = /\b(say it back|the read\b|reading recall|CLIR|quip)\b/i;

   control that begins the run, and Back had no indicator at all.

   The colour depends on the ground, and these buttons appear on both: the door
   draws them on the dark hero panel, every other surface on cream. */
.sl .sl-start:focus-visible,
.sl .sl-brief:focus-visible {
  outline: 2px solid var(--rd-ink);
  outline-offset: 3px;
}
.rd-hero .sl-start:focus-visible,
.rd-hero .sl-brief:focus-visible {
  outline-color: var(--rd-cream);
}
/* 43x44 was under the 44x44 floor, and this is the exit control. */
.sl-brief {
  min-width: 44px;
}

/* Two more hero classes drawn in white for the door's dark panel. On paper they
   were invisible: the drill's own name, and a Back button nobody could see. */
.sl-briefing-page .sl-chip {
  color: var(--rd-ink);
  background: var(--rd-paper);
  border-color: var(--rd-line);
  backdrop-filter: none;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-brief {
  color: var(--rd-ink-2);
  text-decoration-color: var(--rd-line);
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-brief:hover {
  color: var(--rd-ink);
  text-decoration-color: var(--rd-ink-2);
}
/* A heading inside the long version was set larger than the section heading
   above it, so the page read as if the essay outranked its own label. */
.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-essay h3 {
  font-size: 20px;
  margin-top: 28px;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-title {
  color: var(--rd-ink);
  font-size: clamp(30px, 3.4vw, 46px);
  line-height: 1.06;
  letter-spacing: -0.02em;
  margin: 14px 0 0;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-title em {
  color: var(--rd-ink-2);
}
/* .sl-tagline is white with a drop shadow, which is right on the door's dark
   hero and unreadable here: this page sits on paper. The briefing page reuses
   the class for the same line of copy, so it restates the colour rather than
   forking the class. */
.sl-briefing-page .sl-tagline {
  margin: 12px 0 0;
  color: var(--rd-ink-2);
  text-shadow: none;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-tagline strong {
  color: var(--rd-ink);
}
/* The long version, closed by default. */
.sl-bp-more > summary {
  cursor: pointer;
  list-style: none;
.sl-bp-h2 {
  font-family: var(--rd-mono);
  font-size: 12px;
  font-weight: 500;
  text-transform: uppercase;
  letter-spacing: 0.16em;
  margin-bottom: 16px;
  color: var(--rd-ink);
}
.sl-bp-list {
  display: grid;
  gap: 14px;
  list-style: none;
  padding: 0;
  margin: 0;
}
.sl-bp-list li {
  border: 1px solid var(--rd-line, hsl(var(--border)));
  border-radius: 16px;
  padding: 18px 20px;
}
.sl-bp-n {
  display: block;
  font-family: var(--rd-mono);
  font-size: 11px;
  letter-spacing: 0.16em;
  text-transform: uppercase;
  color: var(--rd-ink-3);
  margin-bottom: 6px;
}
.sl-bp-list li strong {
  display: block;
  font-size: 18px;
  margin-bottom: 6px;
}
/* The sentences she has to follow ran past 100 characters a line. The essay
   already holds 68ch; the steps get a measure too. */
.sl-bp-list li p {
  margin: 0;
  line-height: 1.6;
  max-width: 62ch;
}
.sl-bp-foot p {
  margin: 0;
  max-width: 62ch;
  color: var(--rd-ink-2);
}
.sl-bp-fact {
  font-size: 17px;

/* The demonstrations, on the briefing page. Three players in a row on the
   page's own paper, the caption in the mono label face the steps use, the
   frame in the dark indigo the drill runs in. Every token here is defined
   at the top of this file. */
.sl-bp-demos {
  margin: 36px 0 8px;
}
.sl-bp-demos-lede {
  margin: -6px 0 18px;
  max-width: 62ch;
  color: var(--rd-ink-2);
  font-size: var(--bp-body, 1rem);
  line-height: 1.6;
}
.sl-bp-demo-list {
  display: grid;
  grid-template-columns: repeat(3, minmax(0, 1fr));
  gap: 16px;
  list-style: none;
  padding: 0;
  margin: 0;
}
.sl-bp-demo {
  border: 1px solid var(--rd-line);
  border-radius: 16px;
  padding: 10px 10px 12px;
  background: var(--rd-paper);
}
.sl-bp-demo-frame {
  position: relative;
  aspect-ratio: 16 / 9;
  border-radius: 10px;
  overflow: hidden;
  background: var(--rd-indigo);
}
.sl-bp-demo-frame video {
  display: block;
  width: 100%;
  height: 100%;
  object-fit: cover;
}
.sl-bp-demo-frame video:focus-visible {
  outline: 2px solid var(--rd-ink);
  outline-offset: 2px;
}
.sl-bp-demo-cap {
  display: flex;
  align-items: baseline;
  justify-content: space-between;
  gap: 10px;
  margin-top: 10px;
  padding: 0 4px;
}
.sl-bp-demo-label,
.sl-bp-demo-time {
  font-family: var(--rd-mono);
  font-size: var(--bp-label, 0.6875rem);
  letter-spacing: 0.14em;
  text-transform: uppercase;
  color: var(--rd-ink-3);
}
.sl-bp-demo-label {
  color: var(--rd-ink);
}
@media (max-width: 900px) {
  .sl-bp-demo-list {
    grid-template-columns: repeat(2, minmax(0, 1fr));
  }
}
@media (max-width: 640px) {
  .sl-bp-demo-list {
    grid-template-columns: 1fr;
  }
  .sl-bp-demo-cap {
    flex-direction: column;
    gap: 2px;
  }
}
EOF

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Commit, push the branch, and open the PR
command: cd C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu-wt-rrc-demos && rm -f dev-demos.html src/dev-demos.tsx && git status --short && git add -A && git commit -q -m "$(cat <<'EOF'
RRC briefing: watch the four steps done, three real stimuli captioned by test, section and question

Three demonstrations sit under the steps and before the long version: each
one a stimulus from PrepTest 105 Section 2, run exactly as the steps say and
filmed from the student's own eyes (face the page, look away to tell it back,
come back to check, the whole argument from memory with the text gone).

The caption is assembled from numbers, never typed, so a new demo cannot
arrive with the wrong label. Nothing autoplays: the page is quiet until she
presses play, the same rule as the microphone. Three across on desktop, two
at 900px, one on a phone; every token from section-launch.css.

Guarded like the rest of the page: the demos test checks the caption
assembly, the no-autoplay rule, the placement, and the house rules (no em
dash, no internal vocabulary).

Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF
)" && git push -q -u origin feat/rrc-demo-videos 2>&1 | tail -2 && gh pr create --title "RRC briefing: three worked demonstrations, captioned by test, section and question" --body "$(cat <<'EOF'
## What
Adds a **Watch it done** block to the Read, Recall, Colour briefing page: three video demonstrations (PrepTest 105, Section 2, Questions 1 to 3), each captioned `Demo · PrepTest 105 · Section 2 · Question N`, sitting between the four steps and the long version.

The films are the Round 7 first-person cuts from `projects/video`: face the page to read, turn away to tell it back and react, come back to check, then the whole argument from memory with the text gone.

## How
- `content/read-recall-colour-section.demos.ts`: the list, with the caption assembled from numbers so it cannot drift.
- `components/SectionDemos.tsx`: the block. `controls`, `preload="metadata"`, poster, no autoplay, no loop.
- `section-launch.css`: three across, two at 900px, one at 640px; tokens only.
- `public/rrc-demos/`: three 1080p H.264 files (30, 31, 21 MB) plus posters, same convention as the fallacy-flaw bootcamp video.

## Verified
- `SectionBriefingPage.demos.test.tsx` (4) + existing page tests (11) + instructions voice test (5): 20 passing.
- `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.app.json` clean.
- Rendered in the browser: three 269px columns at 1280 wide, captions in the mono label face, posters and video metadata loaded (1920x1080, durations 2:20 / 2:22 / 1:50); one column at 375 wide with no horizontal overflow.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
EOF
)" 2>&1 | tail -2
[/external_agent_tool_call]

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_result]
---
name: lovable-frontend-publish-is-separate
description: A push builds a Lovable preview but never reaches lsatprep.study; publish with deploy_project, wait for the sync, and prove it from the bundle.
metadata:
  type: project
---

Pushing to `main` makes Lovable build the commit — its project record shows a
preview screenshot tagged with the commit sha within minutes — but the published
site at lsatprep.study keeps serving the previous bundle until something calls
`deploy_project` (Lovable MCP, `project_id 53b9a6f2-aba7-4ae4-8dd1-fa5b8aab4d76`,
name `lsatu`). Confirmed 2026-09-08: a fix sat built-but-unpublished for 20
minutes with no error anywhere.

**The sync lags the push.** `deploy_project` publishes whatever Lovable has
ingested, not what is on origin. On 2026-09-09 a deploy fired seconds after a
successful push and shipped the *previous* commit; `get_project` showed
`latest_commit_sha` catching up about three minutes later, and a second deploy
was needed. There is no error and no warning — the deploy reports `pending` and
succeeds, just on the wrong commit.

**Why:** build, sync and publish are three separate steps, and none of them
reports that the others have not happened. A green push reads as shipped.

**How to apply:** before calling `deploy_project`, call `get_project` and check
`latest_commit_sha` equals your HEAD; if it does not, wait and re-check. After
deploying, prove it from the bundle: pick a string **the commit alone**
introduces — not a class name an earlier deploy already shipped, which is how a
stale bundle passed a check on 2026-09-09 — then fetch `https://lsatprep.study/`,
read the `index-*` asset hash out of it, and grep the asset for the marker.
Allow several minutes; the hash changing is the signal a publish landed at all.
Note `deploy_project` ships every unpublished commit on main, not just yours.

Same trap, different half: [[lovable-edge-functions-do-not-deploy-from-push]].
Related: [[push-only-your-own-commits]].
[/external_agent_tool_result]

# Session Handoff — RRC instructional video Round 7 (first-person head turn), and the demos shipped live on the RRC briefing page

## Where it started
Picked up from the Round 6 handoff. Joshua gave a new revision list (stimulus out of the way while reciting, highlight tighter, seamless motion, bubbles off the stimulus, 4K, realistic, first-person head turn) and said "grill me". Four grill questions were answered, then he delegated the rest ("I give you the liberty to do this in the best way possible"). After watching the result he asked for the cards to face the viewer square on, then to put the three cuts on the RRC drill page in LSAT U as demos, live.

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- Head turn is real, from a cylindrical panorama of the same room, not a wider flat photo — `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\video\align\pano\` (`make_config.py`, `build_pano.py`, `depth_pano.py`, `render_views.py`, `fit_left.py`, `fit_views.py` which failed and is kept as evidence). Plate hFOV assumed 66; rot45-3 placed by three hand-read seam features; seam on the door mullion; left bookshelf strip from outpaint-2 for theta < -21.
- One camera model, two consumers — `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\video\src\pov\headTurn.ts` is the truth; `align\export_camera.ts` (run with plain `node`) writes `align\pano\camera.json`; Python refuses to render if its monitor projection disagrees (measured 0.0000 px).
- Turn is 40 deg (bezel-and-margin sliver ~6% of frame at the left edge), half turn 8 deg for card beats, neck pivot 0.09 m for parallax, 4 shutter samples per moving frame. 105 frames at 3840x2160 in `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\video\public\plates\r7\seq\`.
- Screen centred straight ahead (R7-Q3); FACING_HFOV derived from Round 6's accepted monitor size.
- Every overlay is a room object projected through the same camera (`src\pov\World.tsx`, `anchors.ts`), and after his first viewing all of them are billboards (square on to the eye) centred on the turned line of sight (theta 40-42).
- "Look Away" is his verbatim wording, chrome serif italic sign with layered extrusion (`src\pov\LookAway.tsx`; the chrome face and the extrusion are two stacked copies because Chrome paints text-shadow over clipped fills).
- Bubbles, placard, intro cards (`src\pov\Cards.tsx`), Recall column (`src\pov\RecallRoom.tsx`), marker highlighter (`src\pov\Stimulus7.tsx`), room effects: blink, exposure adaptation, peripheral blur, vignette, drift (`src\pov\HeadRoom.tsx`). Beat-to-head rule in `src\pov\headState.ts`: turn away entering a tell/colour/recall run (LEAD 0.55 s off a read), back so the eye lands as the next beat starts.
- Peripheral blur is allowed as an optical effect (his Q3 words), which partially supersedes Round 6's no-blur rule; masking blur on the page stays banned.
- Kling spend: 175 credits (5 images at 35 each), over my own 150 cap; 466 remain. Two outpaints unusable as pixel-preserving glue. No further generation.
- Renders: `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\video\out\r7\instr-q{1,2,3}-4k.mp4` (3840x2160; 139.9 / 141.6 / 109.6 s) plus `-1080` (Q1) and `-720` copies, all sent.
- LSAT U: PR [#114](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/114) squash-merged to main (ec3fe56) and published — `SectionDemos.tsx`, `read-recall-colour-section.demos.ts`, `SectionBriefingPage.demos.test.tsx`, styles appended to `section-launch.css`, 1080p files in `public\rrc-demos\`. "Watch it done" sits between the steps and the long version, captions "Demo · PrepTest 105 · Section 2 · Question N".
- A Lovable plugin rewrote `supabase\functions\mcp\index.ts` during `npm ci` in the worktree and it was swept into the first commit; force push was declined by Joshua, so a second commit restored the file before merge.
- Vault launch config gained `lsatu-rrc-demos-dev` (port 8093) in `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\.claude\launch.json`.

## Key files for next session
- `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\brainstorms\2026-09-10-rrc-instructional-video-round7.md` — read first: Q1-Q4, build plan, build log, his feedback, open flags.
- `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\video\README.md` — Round 7 section: pipeline commands in order and the traps.
- `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\video\src\pov\headTurn.ts`, `headState.ts`, `anchors.ts`, `World.tsx`, `HeadRoom.tsx`, `POVInstructional.tsx` (Round 6 version kept as `POVInstructional.r6.tsx.bak`).
- `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu-wt-rrc-demos\src\features\activity-centre\components\SectionDemos.tsx` and `..\content\read-recall-colour-section.demos.ts`.
